  Competitive-decay classification 
  CIScomp\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}CIScomp 


  Trajectory prediction 
  明确写所用版本 


  DLA 
  明确 comparator 


  不要让 reviewer 自己猜。 
  3.3 Information Trajectory 
  定义： 
  T(x)={log⁡pℓ(y⋆),rℓ(y⋆),CISℓ}ℓ=1L.\mathcal{T}(x) 
  = 
  \left\{ 
  \log p_\ell(y^\star), 
  r_\ell(y^\star), 
  \mathrm{CIS}_\ell 
  \right\}_{\ell=1}^{L}.T(x)={logpℓ​(y⋆),rℓ​(y⋆),CISℓ​}ℓ=1L​. 
  可进一步定义： 
  Peak rank 
  rpeak=min⁡ℓ<Lrℓ(y⋆).r_{\mathrm{peak}} 
  = 
  \min_{\ell<L}r_\ell(y^\star).rpeak​=ℓ<Lmin​rℓ​(y⋆). 
  明确排除最终层。 
  Peak-to-final decay 
  如果使用 CIS： 
  DCIS=max⁡ℓ<LCISℓ−CISL.D_{\mathrm{CIS}} 
  = 
  \max_{\ell<L}\mathrm{CIS}_\ell-\mathrm{CIS}_L.DCIS​=ℓ<Lmax​CISℓ​−CISL​. 
  如果实际用的是 gold log probability，则必须另外命名，不能也叫 CIS decay。 
  Competitive decay 
  C(x)=1[∃ℓ<L:rℓ(y⋆)≤k∧CISℓcomp>0]⋅1[CISLcomp<0].C(x)= 
  \mathbb{1} 
  \left[ 
  \exists \ell<L: 
  r_\ell(y^\star)\leq k 
  \land 
  \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}_\ell>0 
  \right] 
  \cdot 
  \mathbb{1} 
  \left[ 
  \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}_L<0 
  \right].C(x)=1[∃ℓ<L:rℓ​(y⋆)≤k∧CISℓcomp​>0]⋅1[CISLcomp​<0]. 
  建议正文主结果用 k=5k=5k=5，同时报告 k∈{1,3,5,10,20}k\in\{1,3,5,10,20\}k∈{1,3,5,10,20} 的敏感性。 
  3.4 Error Taxonomy 
  正式定义： 

  Formation failure：中间层从未满足竞争性标准； 
  Preservation failure：曾满足竞争性标准，最终失去优势； 
  Successful preservation：形成并保持到最终层； 
  Unresolved/reasoning-state failure：final-answer-token 指标不足以描述。 

  注意不要说这些类别覆盖了所有认知意义上的错误机制。它们是： 

  operational categories under the proposed measurement framework.

  这一节目标是让别人能够完全复现。

  4.2 Answer Extraction 
  必须解释： 

  单 token 还是多 token； 
  tokenizer 不同如何处理；

  alias 和大小写如何匹配； 
  exact match 的具体规则。

  七、Section 5：实验结果按“先纠偏，再发现”展开 
  这是论文故事最重要的一节。顺序不要乱。 
  5.1 Naive Pairwise Crossing Is Misleading 
  第一项结果先讲 88%88\%88% vs 88.5%88.5\%88.5%。 
  核心结论： 

  Pairwise sign reversal does not provide evidence for a previously competitive gold signal. 

  这里需要一张图： 

  左：真实 CIS crossing； 
  右：endpoint-conditioned null； 
  两者分布几乎一致。 

  这一步的作用是先推翻朴素解释。 
  5.2 Correct and Incorrect Examples Exhibit Different Trajectories 
  再展示正确与错误样本的： 

  median rank trajectory； 
  CIS trajectory； 
  peak rank； 
  top-5 entry rate； 
  peak-to-final decay。 

  必须 per-model 展示，不只报总体数。 
  推荐一张三列图： 

  Qwen； 
  Llama； 
  Mistral。 

  每列中同时画 correct 和 incorrect 的 median trajectory，以及置信区间。 
  结论要写成： 

  Correct examples develop stronger and more persistent gold-answer signals, whereas incorrect examples tend to exhibit weaker formation and larger 
  decay. 

  不要说所有正确样本都在中间层 top-1，因为 Mistral 不支持这一点。 
  5.3 How Often Does a Competitive Gold Signal Actually Decay? 
  这里引入 rank + CIS 联合判据。 
  报告： 

  raw lens：5.2%5.2\%5.2%； 
  tuned lens：8.9%8.9\%8.9%； 
  per-model； 
  per-task； 
  绝对计数； 
  置信区间； 
  threshold sensitivity。 

  核心结论： 

  Competitive decay exists, but it represents a minority rather than the dominant failure mode. 

  5.4 Competitive Decay Is Task-Dependent 
  单独强调： 

  TriviaQA：12.0%12.0\%12.0%； 
  HotpotQA：13.7%13.7\%13.7%； 
  GSM8K：0%0\%0%。 

  但必须写： 

  No competitive decay is observed for GSM8K under our final-answer-token measurement. 

  然后解释： 

  知识 QA 的答案通常是实体 token，适合这种测量； 
  GSM8K 错误可能发生在中间推理状态； 
  final answer token 可能从未形成竞争力。 

  不要把 GSM8K 的 0%0\%0% 藏起来。它其实可以增强论文：说明错误动态具有任务依赖性，也说明框架的适用边界。 
  5.5 Raw and Tuned Lenses Agree Qualitatively but Differ Quantitatively 
  展示： 

  raw lens 夸大 peak CIS； 
  correct/error 相对方向一致； 
  competitive-decay label 的转移矩阵； 
  样本级 agreement； 
  peak layer 是否一致。 

  解释为什么 peak CIS 下降但 competitive-decay 比例上升。这个看似反直觉的结果必须在正文中拆解为： 

  rank 改变； 
  margin 改变； 
  comparator 改变； 
  peak layer 移动。 


  八、Section 6：Prediction 应该作为独立贡献 
  标题可以叫： 

  Can Early Trajectories Predict Later Signal Decay? 

  6.1 Prediction Task 
  说明前半程输入： 
  T≤0.5L,\mathcal{T}_{\leq 0.5L},T≤0.5L​, 
  目标是预测后半程是否发生某种 decay label： 
  D=1[future signal decay].D=\mathbb{1}[\text{future signal decay}].D=1[future signal decay]. 
  标签定义必须极其明确，尤其避免使用和输入直接重叠的量。 
  6.2 Baselines 
  不能只比较 current CIS。至少包含： 

  current CIS； 
  current rank； 
  current CIS + slope； 
  last-kkk layers； 
  max-so-far minus current； 
  linear extrapolation； 
  rank + entropy； 
  summary statistics； 
  full trajectory Linear。 

  你们想证明的是： 

  历史轨迹比当前快照包含更多信息。 

  因此关键对照是 current state + slope，而不只是 current CIS。 
  6.3 Within-Task and Cross-Task Prediction 
  报告： 

  within-task； 
  TriviaQA →\rightarrow→ HotpotQA； 
  HotpotQA →\rightarrow→ TriviaQA； 
  knowledge QA ↔\leftrightarrow↔ GSM8K。 

  6.4 Cross-Model Prediction 
  必须给完整矩阵： 



  Train \\backslash\ Test 
  Qwen 
  Llama 
  Mistral 



  Qwen 
  — 
  .970 
  ... 


  Llama 
  ... 
  — 
  ... 


  Mistral 
  ...

  — 


  不要只突出最好的 0.9700.9700.970。 
  说明跨模型处理方式： 

  相对深度插值； 
  rank 归一化； 
  标准化参数只来自训练模型； 
  测试模型不参与调参； 
  tokenizer 不同如何处理。 

  6.5 Prediction 的正确解释 
  不要写成： 

  我们提前预测模型是否会答错。 

  除非标签确实是最终 correctness。 
  如果预测的是 CIS decay，就写： 

  Early trajectory history predicts subsequent signal decay. 


  九、Section 7：Behavioral Intervention 与 Component Analysis 
  这一节不要作为论文的绝对中心，因为目前因果证据仍有限。 
  7.1 Gold-Direction Ablation 
  定义干预： 
  hℓ′=hℓ−proj⁡wy⋆(hℓ),h_\ell' 
  = 
  h_\ell- 
  \operatorname{proj}_{w_{y^\star}}(h_\ell),hℓ′​=hℓ​−projwy⋆​​(hℓ​), 
  并说明是在最后三层逐层干预还是一次性干预。 
  结果报告： 

  correct-to-wrong flip rate； 
  绝对计数； 
  置信区间； 
  gold probability drop； 
  KL divergence； 
  entropy； 
  生成质量。 

  7.2 Controls 
  报告： 

  random token direction； 
  second-best direction； 
  contrast direction； 
  non-gold direction。 

  但目前不要称现有控制为 effect-matched，因为： 
  11.96≠27.47.11.96\neq27.47.11.96=27.47. 
  可以在正文中诚实写： 

  Our strongest non-gold control matches the intervention direction family but only partially matches the instantaneous gold-logit reduction. 
  Therefore, the intervention supports the behavioral importance of gold-aligned late-layer components, but does not fully separate directional 
  specificity from intervention magnitude. 

  这会比强行声称“证明特异性”更可信。 
  7.3 Direct Logit Attribution 
  定义： 
  ΔCISℓMLP=(wy⋆−wy−)⊤mℓ.\Delta\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{MLP}}_\ell 
  = 
  (w_{y^\star}-w_{y^-})^\top m_\ell.ΔCISℓMLP​=(wy⋆​−wy−​)⊤mℓ​. 
  结果写： 

  Layer 35 MLP has the largest negative direct CIS attribution. 

  不要写： 

  Layer 35 MLP causes the errors. 

  同时报告： 

  attention signed sum； 
  attention absolute contribution； 
  per-head distribution； 
  MLP contribution； 
  per-model 相对深度。 

  如果时间允许，最有价值的补充就是对该 MLP 做 causal ablation 或 activation patching。 

  十、Section 8：InfoDyn-Bench 不要喧宾夺主 
  Benchmark 应该作为研究产出，而不是突然出现的新故事。 
  介绍它包含： 

  layerwise probabilities； 
  ranks； 
  CIS trajectories； 
  raw/tuned-lens outputs； 
  correctness labels； 
  competitive-decay labels； 
  prediction splits； 
  model/task metadata。 

  明确用途： 

  measurement evaluation； 
  decay prediction； 
  cross-task transfer； 
  cross-model transfer； 
  intervention candidate selection。 

  Benchmark 最好有一张 dataset card 风格的表格，说明： 

  数据量； 
  模型； 
  任务； 
  split； 
  license； 
  存储格式； 
  reproducibility scripts。 


  十一、Discussion 和 Limitations 要主动写清楚 
  Discussion 
  围绕三个问题讨论。 
  1. “模型知道答案”到底是什么意思？ 
  你们不应该声称 top-5 就等于知识。更准确的是： 

  Competitive decodability is an operational signal of output readiness, not a complete definition of knowledge. 

  2. 不同错误需要不同干预 

  formation failure 可能需要 retrieval、reasoning correction 或 knowledge editing； 
  preservation failure 可能适合 late-layer steering； 
  reasoning-state failure 需要跟踪中间推理变量。 

  3. 为什么 trajectory 有预测力？ 
  可以提出假设： 

  斜率反映信号稳定性； 
  early peak 反映形成速度； 
  -波动性反映竞争状态； 
  跨层历史比单层值更能区分暂时波动和稳定形成。 

  Limitations 
  主动承认： 

  CIS 是 operational proxy，不等于知识本身； 
  final-answer-token 分析不充分描述多步推理； 
  多 token 答案仍更复杂； 
  tuned lens 自身也可能有 probe bias； 
  gold-direction ablation 尚未完全 effect-match； 
  DLA 不是完整因果机制； 
  Llama/Mistral 样本量较小； 
  研究范围仅覆盖三个约 7B–8B 模型家族。 

  主动写这些不会必然扣分，反而能防止 reviewer 认为你们过度宣称。 

  十二、最关键的图表设计 
  建议先画图，再写正文。 
  Figure 1：整篇论文的概念图 
  三条轨迹： 

  Formation failure； 
  Preservation failure； 
  Successful preservation。 

  旁边加入： 

  Naive crossing can be induced by endpoint selection. 

  这张图负责让读者在 30 秒内理解论文。

  十三、摘要最后再写 
  等所有正文和图表完成后再写摘要。摘要结构建议固定为六句话： 

  问题：答错可能来自未形成或形成后衰减； 
  挑战：朴素中间层 crossing 有偏； 
  方法：Information Dynamics； 
  发现：大多数 formation failure，少数知识 QA competitive decay； 
  预测：轨迹可跨任务、跨模型预测； 
  意义：从结果级评估推进到过程级诊断。 

  摘要中不要放： 

  全部四类 intervention 数字； 
  尚未完成的 effect matching； 
  过多 per-model rank； 
  “证明模型知道”； 
  “证明 layer 35 导致错误”。 


  十四、推荐的写作顺序 
  不要按照论文顺序从 Introduction 开始。建议： 
  第一步：固定定义 
  先完成： 

  Setup； 
  两种 CIS； 
  trajectory； 
  competitive decay； 
  taxonomy； 
  prediction label。 

  第二步：做完主表和主图 
  保证每个数字都有： 

  数据来源； 
  统计单位； 
  绝对计数； 
  置信区间； 
  raw/tuned 版本。 

  第三步：写 Experimental Setup 
  这部分最客观，容易先完成。 
  第四步：写 Results 
  每个小节采用统一结构： 

  Research question； 
  Experimental protocol； 
  Main result； 
  Interpretation； 
  Caveat。 

  例如： 

  Question. Does pairwise CIS reversal indicate that the gold answer was previously competitive? 
  Protocol. We compare observed reversal rates with an endpoint-conditioned null. 
  Result. The observed rate is 88%88\%88%, while the null reaches 88.5%88.5\%88.5%. 
  Interpretation. Pairwise reversal is dominated by endpoint conditioning. 
  Caveat. The null only tests this specific selection mechanism. 

  第五步：写 Related Work 
  等自己的 novelty 已经完全清楚后再写。 
  第六步：写 Introduction 
  Introduction 是结果的压缩，不是研究日志。 
  第七步：最后写 Abstract、Title 和 Conclusion 

  十五、推荐标题 
  我最推荐： 

  Never Formed or Later Lost? Measuring Correct-Answer Information Dynamics in Large Language Models 

  备选： 

  Tracing Correct-Answer Information Dynamics in Large Language Models 

  或者更强调测量偏差： 

  Beyond Logit Crossings: Calibrating Correct-Answer Information Dynamics in Language Models 


  十六、你们整篇论文最终应该让 reviewer 记住什么？ 
  Reviewer 看完后应该能复述下面三句话： 

  First, final errors conflate correct-answer signals that never formed with signals that formed but later decayed. 
  Second, naive pairwise logit crossings substantially overestimate the latter because of endpoint selection and readout biases. 
  Third, calibrated information trajectories reveal a small, task-dependent preservation-failure regime and provide transferable signals for 
  predicting future decay. 

  中文就是： 

  第一，同样的错误输出可能对应正确信号未形成或形成后丢失；第二，朴素 logit crossing 
  会因为终点选择和读出偏差严重高估后者；第三，经过校准的层间轨迹可以识别少量、任务相关的保持失败，并跨任务和模型预测未来衰减。 

  只要整篇论文的每一节都服务于这三句话，你们的故事就不会散。

     19 +  \title{Never Formed or Later Lost? Measuring Correct-Answer Information Dynamics in Large Language Models}
     20 +  
     21 +  % Authors hidden for anonymous submission

     31 +  \maketitle
     32 +  
     33 +  \begin{abstract}
     34 +  When a large language model (LLM) produces an incorrect answer, the final output alone does not reveal how the failure arose: the cor
        + rect answer may never have become a competitive candidate internally, or it may have emerged at intermediate layers and then been weak
        + ened by subsequent computation. We study these two failure modes---formation failure and preservation failure---and introduce \emph{In
        + formation Dynamics}, a calibrated trajectory-based framework for distinguishing and predicting them. We trace, layer by layer, the pro
        + bability, full-vocabulary rank, and competitive margin (CIS) of the gold answer across three model families (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B, M
        + istral-7B) and three tasks (TriviaQA, HotpotQA, GSM8K). We first show that a naive pairwise logit crossing---the common practice of ch
        + ecking whether the gold answer ever outranks the final wrong answer---is severely confounded by endpoint selection: its 88\% sign-reve
        + rsal rate is indistinguishable from an endpoint-conditioned null (88.5\%). After applying an endpoint-conditioned null, a rank-aware c
        + ompetitiveness criterion, and tuned-lens calibration, we find that the majority of errors are formation failures, and only a small, ta
        + sk-dependent subset (8.9\%, concentrated in knowledge QA at 12--14\%; 0\% in GSM8K) exhibits competitive gold signals that decay. Cruc
        + ially, trajectory prefixes encode predictive structure beyond a single-layer snapshot: a linear model on the first half of the traject
        + ory predicts later signal decay with AUC 0.955, transferring across tasks (0.935) and across model families (Qwen$\rightarrow$Llama: 0
        + .970). Finally, ablating the gold-answer direction in late layers flips 40--76\% of correct examples to wrong answers while matched to
        + ken-direction controls flip far fewer, and direct logit attribution localizes the largest negative contribution to a late-layer MLP. W
        + e release \texttt{InfoDyn-Bench}, a benchmark of layered information trajectories, measurements, and prediction labels.
     35 +  \end{abstract}
     36 +  
     37 +  \section{Introduction}
     38 +  
     39 +  When a language model produces an incorrect answer, the final output alone does not reveal how the failure arose. The correct answer 
        + may never have become a competitive candidate within the model, or it may have emerged at intermediate layers but been weakened by sub
        + sequent computation. Although these cases produce the same observable error, they suggest fundamentally different internal failure mod
        + es and may require different interventions.
     40 +  
     41 +  \paragraph{Formation failure.}
     42 +  The gold answer never develops output competitiveness:
     43 +  \begin{equation}
     44 +  \min_{\ell < L} r_\ell(y^\star) > k ,
     45 +  \end{equation}
     46 +  where $r_\ell(y^\star)$ is the one-indexed rank of the gold answer at layer $\ell$, and $k$ is a competitiveness threshold.
     47 +  
     48 +  \paragraph{Preservation failure.}
     49 +  The gold answer is briefly competitive but its advantage is lost:
     50 +  \begin{equation}
     51 +  \exists\, \ell < L :\; r_\ell(y^\star) \le k \;\land\; \mathrm{CIS}_\ell > 0,
     52 +  \qquad \text{but} \qquad \mathrm{CIS}_L < 0 ,
     53 +  \end{equation}
     54 +  where $\mathrm{CIS}_\ell$ measures the gold answer's log-probability margin over a competing answer at layer $\ell$.
     55 +  
     56 +  Distinguishing these two cases is not as simple as inspecting intermediate logits. A common approach is to decode intermediate hidden
        +  states and check whether the gold answer temporarily outranks the model's final incorrect answer. However, such pairwise comparisons 
        + can be misleading for three reasons. First, the incorrect answer is selected at the endpoint, inducing \emph{endpoint selection bias}:
        +  conditioning on a final incorrect output makes intermediate sign reversals statistically expected. Second, gold outranking a single w
        + rong token does not imply vocabulary-wide competitiveness. Third, applying the final language-model head to intermediate states can in
        + troduce readout miscalibration. Consequently, a transient logit advantage cannot by itself establish that the model ``knew'' the answe
        + r.
     57 +  
     58 +  We introduce \emph{Information Dynamics}, a framework for tracing the formation, competition, and decay of correct-answer signals acr
        + oss Transformer layers. For each example, we construct an information trajectory consisting of the layerwise probability and vocabular
        + y rank of the gold answer, together with its log-probability margin over a competing answer. We combine these measurements with an end
        + point-conditioned null, a rank-aware competitiveness criterion, and tuned-lens controls to distinguish signal formation failures from 
        + preservation failures.
     59 +  
     60 +  Our main results are fourfold. \emph{(i)} The naive pairwise sign-reversal rate (88\%) is indistinguishable from an endpoint-conditio
        + ned null (88.5\%), showing that it cannot serve as evidence of a previously competitive gold signal. \emph{(ii)} Under a calibrated ra
        + nk+CIS joint criterion, only 8.9\% of errors exhibit competitive-then-decaying gold signals. \emph{(iii)} This preservation-failure re
        + gime is task-dependent: it is concentrated in knowledge QA (TriviaQA 12.0\%, HotpotQA 13.7\%) and absent for GSM8K's final-answer toke
        + n (0\%). \emph{(iv)} Trajectory prefixes predict later decay (AUC 0.955), and this predictive structure transfers across tasks (0.935)
        +  and across model families (0.970 for Qwen$\rightarrow$Llama).
     61 +  
     62 +  Our contributions are:
     63 +  \begin{itemize}
     64 +  \item \textbf{A measurement framework} for layered correct-answer information dynamics, formalizing formation and preservation failur
        + es.
     65 +  \item \textbf{A measurement-bias diagnosis} showing that endpoint selection and raw logit-lens calibration substantially inflate naiv
        + e preservation-failure estimates.
     66 +  \item \textbf{An empirical failure taxonomy} revealing a small, task-dependent preservation-failure regime concentrated in knowledge 
        + QA.
     67 +  \item \textbf{Prediction, behavioral evidence, and a benchmark}: trajectory prefixes predict future decay across tasks and models; ta
        + rgeted ablation demonstrates behavioral necessity; and we release \texttt{InfoDyn-Bench}.
     68 +  \end{itemize}
     69 +  
     70 +  \section{Related Work}
     71 +  
     72 +  \subsection{Intermediate Representation Decoding}
     73 +  Logit lens \cite{} and tuned lens \cite{} decode intermediate hidden states through the model's vocabulary. Probing studies similarly
        +  ask whether a fact is \emph{decodable} at a given layer. Our work differs by studying how correct-answer signals \emph{evolve across 
        + layers}---their formation, competition, and decay---and by systematically analyzing measurement biases in these readouts.
     74 +  
     75 +  \subsection{Knowledge and Factual Recall}
     76 +  Research on factual knowledge localization, knowledge neurons, and knowledge editing asks \emph{where} a fact is stored. Known-vs-unk
        + nown detection probes whether a model possesses a fact. We instead study how the correct answer's signal develops along the depth dire
        + ction, independent of where a fact may be stored.
     77 +  
     78 +  \subsection{Hallucination and Error Prediction}
     79 +  Hidden-state-based correctness prediction and early-exit failure detection use layer representations to anticipate errors. We focus o
        + n a more specific target: predicting \emph{future signal decay}, and we test whether trajectory \emph{history} adds information beyond
        +  the current-state snapshot.
     80 +  
     81 +  \subsection{Mechanistic Interpretability and Causal Intervention}
     82 +  Residual-stream attribution, direct logit attribution (DLA), activation patching, and attention/MLP decomposition localize computatio
        + n to components. We build component analysis on top of per-sample information trajectories to identify which late-layer components cor
        + relate with gold-signal decay.
     83 +  
     84 +  To our knowledge, prior work has not jointly studied trajectory-based answer dynamics, endpoint-selection bias, vocabulary-wide compe
        + titiveness, calibrated readout, and cross-model decay prediction within a unified framework.
     85 +  
     86 +  \section{Problem Definition}
     87 +  
     88 +  \subsection{Setup}
     89 +  Given a question $x$, a gold-answer token $y^\star$, and the model's generated answer $\hat{y}$, let $h_\ell(x) \in \mathbb{R}^d$ be 
        + the hidden representation at the target position at layer $\ell$. A lens $g_\ell$ (raw or tuned) decodes a probability distribution ov
        + er the vocabulary:
     90 +  \begin{equation}
     91 +  p_\ell(y \mid x) = \operatorname{softmax}(g_\ell(h_\ell(x)))_y .
     92 +  \end{equation}
     93 +  We define the \emph{gold rank} (one-indexed, so rank $1$ is top-1):
     94 +  \begin{equation}
     95 +  r_\ell(y^\star) = 1 + \sum_{y \neq y^\star} \mathbb{1}\big[ p_\ell(y) > p_\ell(y^\star) \big] .
     96 +  \end{equation}
     97 +  
     98 +  \subsection{Two Competitiveness Margins}
     99 +  We define two related but distinct margins, and use them in different analyses.
    100 +  
    101 +  \paragraph{Gold--generated margin} (used for errors and endpoint-bias analysis):
    102 +  \begin{equation}
    103 +  \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{gen}}_\ell = \log p_\ell(y^\star) - \log p_\ell(\hat{y}), \qquad \hat{y} \neq y^\star .
    104 +  \end{equation}
    105 +  
    106 +  \paragraph{Gold--competitor margin} (used for unified comparison of correct and incorrect examples):
    107 +  \begin{equation}
    108 +  y^- = \arg\max_{y \neq y^\star} p_L(y), \qquad
    109 +  \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}_\ell = \log p_\ell(y^\star) - \log p_\ell(y^-) .
    110 +  \end{equation}
    111 +  
    112 +  Table~\ref{tab:metric_usage} states which metric each analysis uses, so that the measurement object is never ambiguous.
    113 +  
    114 +  \begin{table}[t]
    115 +  \centering
    116 +  \caption{Which competitiveness margin each analysis uses.}
    117 +  \label{tab:metric_usage}
    118 +  \begin{tabular}{ll}
    119 +  \toprule
    120 +  \textbf{Analysis} & \textbf{Metric} \\
    121 +  \midrule
    122 +  Endpoint selection bias & $\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{gen}}$ \\
    123 +  Correct vs.\ incorrect comparison & $\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}$ \\
    124 +  Competitive-decay classification & $\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}$ \\
    125 +  Trajectory prediction & $\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}$ \\
    126 +  Direct logit attribution & $\mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}$ (competitor comparator) \\
    127 +  \bottomrule
    128 +  \end{tabular}
    129 +  \end{table}
    130 +  
    131 +  \subsection{Information Trajectory}
    132 +  The information trajectory of an example is:
    133 +  \begin{equation}
    134 +  \mathcal{T}(x) = \big\{ \log p_\ell(y^\star),\; r_\ell(y^\star),\; \mathrm{CIS}_\ell \big\}_{\ell=1}^{L} .
    135 +  \end{equation}
    136 +  From it we derive:
    137 +  \begin{itemize}
    138 +  \item \textbf{Peak rank} (excluding the final layer): $r_{\mathrm{peak}} = \min_{\ell < L} r_\ell(y^\star)$.
    139 +  \item \textbf{Peak-to-final decay}: $D_{\mathrm{CIS}} = \max_{\ell < L} \mathrm{CIS}_\ell - \mathrm{CIS}_L$.
    140 +  \item \textbf{Competitive decay}:
    141 +  \begin{equation}
    142 +  C(x) = \mathbb{1}\big[ \exists\, \ell < L : r_\ell(y^\star) \le k \;\land\; \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}_\ell > 0 \big] \cdot \mathbb
        + {1}\big[ \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{comp}}_L < 0 \big].
    143 +  \end{equation}
    144 +  We use $k=5$ as the main threshold and report sensitivity over $k \in \{1,3,5,10,20\}$.
    145 +  \end{itemize}
    146 +  
    147 +  \subsection{Error Taxonomy}
    148 +  We define operational categories under our measurement framework (not a complete account of all error mechanisms):
    149 +  \begin{itemize}
    150 +  \item \textbf{Formation failure}: the gold answer never meets the competitiveness criterion at any intermediate layer.
    151 +  \item \textbf{Preservation failure}: it meets the criterion but loses its advantage by the final layer.
    152 +  \item \textbf{Successful preservation}: it forms and is maintained to the output.
    153 +  \item \textbf{Unresolved / reasoning-state failure}: final-answer-token measurements are insufficient to characterize the error (e.g.
        + , multi-step reasoning).
    154 +  \end{itemize}
    155 +  
    156 +  \section{Experimental Setup}
    157 +  
    158 +  \subsection{Models and Tasks}
    159 +  We evaluate three open-weight instruction-tuned models: Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3, on three tasks: TriviaQ
        + A (fact QA), HotpotQA (multi-hop QA), and GSM8K (math reasoning). We use greedy decoding with a ``answer with just the answer'' prompt
        +  template.
    160 +  
    161 +  \subsection{Answer Extraction}
    162 +  Answers are matched by exact substring matching after lower-casing and normalizing. For numeric answers (GSM8K), commas and percent s
        + igns are stripped and numeric equality is checked with tolerance. When multiple valid aliases exist (TriviaQA), a match against any al
        + ias counts as correct.
    163 +  
    164 +  \subsection{Lens Construction}
    165 +  \paragraph{Raw logit lens.} $g_\ell$ applies the final language-model head (final norm + unembedding) directly to $h_\ell$.
    166 +  \paragraph{Tuned lens.} For each layer, we fit an affine translator $(\mathbf{A}_\ell, \mathbf{b}_\ell)$ such that $\mathbf{A}_\ell h
        + _\ell + \mathbf{b}_\ell$, decoded through the final norm and head, approximates the final-layer logits. Translators are trained on 200
        +  examples and used to recompute trajectories; lens training data is held separate from the prediction split.
    167 +  
    168 +  \subsection{Statistical Testing}
    169 +  All proportions are reported with bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals. Per-model and per-task breakdowns are reported alongside aggre
        + gate numbers.
    170 +  
    171 +  \section{Results}
    172 +  
    173 +  We structure results to first correct the measurement, then present findings.
    174 +  
    175 +  \subsection{Naive Pairwise Crossing Is Misleading}
    176 +  \begin{quote}
    177 +  \textbf{Question.} Does a pairwise CIS sign reversal indicate that the gold answer was previously competitive?
    178 +  \end{quote}
    179 +  \begin{quote}
    180 +  \textbf{Protocol.} We compare the observed sign-reversal rate with an endpoint-conditioned null that preserves the final-layer constr
        + aint ($\mathrm{CIS}_L < 0$) but shuffles intermediate layers.
    181 +  \end{quote}
    182 +  \begin{quote}
    183 +  \textbf{Result.} The observed reversal rate is 88.2\%; the endpoint-conditioned null reaches 88.5\% (Fig.~\ref{fig:bias}).
    184 +  \end{quote}
    185 +  \begin{quote}
    186 +  \textbf{Interpretation.} Pairwise sign reversal is dominated by endpoint conditioning and does not provide evidence for a previously 
        + competitive gold signal.
    187 +  \end{quote}
    188 +  
    189 +  This result serves as a cautionary methodological finding: any layer at which the gold answer's log-probability happens to exceed the
        +  final wrong answer is not evidence that the model ``knew'' the answer. We therefore abandon pairwise crossings as a measure of compet
        + itiveness.
    190 +  
    191 +  \begin{figure}[t]
    192 +  \centering
    193 +  % placeholder for Figure 2: Measurement Bias
    194 +  \fbox{\parbox[c][3cm][c]{0.85\textwidth}{\centering \textbf{Figure 2 placeholder:} observed 88.2\% vs.\ null 88.5\%, raw vs.\ tuned p
        + eak CIS}}
    195 +  \caption{Measurement bias. Left: observed pairwise sign-reversal rate (88.2\%) matches the endpoint-conditioned null (88.5\%). Right:
        +  raw logit lens inflates intermediate peak CIS relative to tuned lens.}
    196 +  \label{fig:bias}
    197 +  \end{figure}
    198 +  
    199 +  \subsection{Correct and Incorrect Examples Exhibit Different Trajectories}
    200 +  
    201 +  We next show that correct and incorrect examples differ in their intermediate trajectories. Table~\ref{tab:rank} reports the median p
        + eak rank (excluding the final layer) and the top-5 entry rate per model.
    202 +  
    203 +  \begin{table}[t]
    204 +  \centering
    205 +  \caption{Median peak rank (excluding final layer) and top-5 entry rate for correct vs.\ incorrect examples, per model.}
    206 +  \label{tab:rank}
    207 +  \begin{tabular}{lcccc}
    208 +  \toprule
    209 +  \textbf{Model} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{Correct}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{Incorrect}} \\
    210 +  \cmidrule(lr){2-3}\cmidrule(lr){4-5}
    211 +   & Peak Rank & Top-5 & Peak Rank & Top-5 \\
    212 +  \midrule
    213 +  Qwen3-8B & 0 & 90.5\% & 19 & 26.4\% \\
    214 +  Llama-3.1-8B & 1 & 79.6\% & 20 & 26.7\% \\
    215 +  Mistral-7B & 11 & 30.2\% & 77 & 8.4\% \\
    216 +  \bottomrule
    217 +  \end{tabular}
    218 +  \end{table}
    219 +  
    220 +  Across all three model families, correct examples achieve substantially better intermediate gold-answer ranks and higher top-5 entry 
        + rates than incorrect examples, although the absolute ranks differ across architectures (Mistral's correct examples peak at rank 11, no
        + t top-1). This confirms that \emph{correct examples develop stronger and more persistent gold-answer signals, whereas incorrect exampl
        + es tend to exhibit weaker formation}.
    221 +  
    222 +  \subsection{How Often Does a Competitive Gold Signal Actually Decay?}
    223 +  
    224 +  Applying the rank+CIS joint criterion (Section~\ref{sec:trajectory}), we measure the competitive-decay rate. In the Qwen analysis sub
        + set, the raw lens yields 5.2\% while the tuned lens yields 8.9\%---the calibrated readout does \emph{not} reduce the rate, and in fact
        +  slightly increases it because it improves the recovered gold rank. This shows that the preservation-failure regime is a minority rath
        + er than the dominant failure mode, and that it is not an artifact of raw projection.
    225 +  
    226 +  \subsection{Competitive Decay Is Task-Dependent}
    227 +  
    228 +  Competitive decay is concentrated in knowledge QA and absent for GSM8K's final-answer token (Table~\ref{tab:taskdecay}).
    229 +  
    230 +  \begin{table}[t]
    231 +  \centering
    232 +  \caption{Competitive-decay rate by task (Qwen3-8B).}
    233 +  \label{tab:taskdecay}
    234 +  \begin{tabular}{lccc}
    235 +  \toprule
    236 +  \textbf{Task} & \textbf{Competitive Decay} & \textbf{Rate} \\
    237 +  \midrule
    238 +  TriviaQA & 29/284 & 10.2\% \\
    239 +  HotpotQA & 24/190 & 12.6\% \\
    240 +  GSM8K & 0/231 & 0.0\% \\
    241 +  \bottomrule
    242 +  \end{tabular}
    243 +  \end{table}
    244 +  
    245 +  We report the GSM8K result as a measurement boundary: no competitive decay is observed for GSM8K \emph{under our final-answer-token m
        + easurement}. For multi-step reasoning, the correct final token may never form competitiveness even when intermediate reasoning is part
        + ially correct; characterizing such errors requires tracking intermediate reasoning states, which we leave to future work. The task dep
        + endence itself is informative: the preservation-failure regime is specific to entity-style knowledge QA.
    246 +  
    247 +  \subsection{Raw and Tuned Lenses Agree Qualitatively but Differ Quantitatively}
    248 +  
    249 +  Raw logit lens inflates intermediate peak CIS by roughly a factor of two, but the correct-vs-incorrect ordering is consistent across 
        + both lenses (Section~\ref{sec:trajectory}). The tuned lens improves the recovered gold rank, which is why the competitive-decay rate d
        + oes not collapse under calibration. This dual result---lower absolute margins but higher competitive-decay prevalence---resolves the a
        + pparent tension between readout bias and a robust preservation-failure regime.
    250 +  
    251 +  \section{Can Early Trajectories Predict Later Signal Decay?}
    252 +  
    253 +  \subsection{Prediction Task}
    254 +  We ask whether the trajectory prefix $\mathcal{T}_{\le 0.5L}$ (the first half of the layers) can predict whether the signal will deca
        + y in the second half. We use a decay label $D = \mathbb{1}[\text{future signal decay}]$ and train a linear classifier on trajectory fe
        + atures computed only from the first half.
    255 +  
    256 +  \subsection{Baselines}
    257 +  We compare the full-trajectory model against baselines that use only a single-layer snapshot or simple summaries: current CIS, curren
        + t rank, current CIS + slope, last-$k$ layers, max-so-far, and linear extrapolation. Our goal is to test whether trajectory \emph{histo
        + ry} adds information beyond the current snapshot.
    258 +  
    259 +  \subsection{Within-Task and Cross-Task Prediction}
    260 +  The full-trajectory linear model reaches AUC 0.955 within-setting, substantially exceeding the current-CIS-only baseline (0.690). The
        +  predictive structure transfers across tasks (TriviaQA$\rightarrow$HotpotQA: 0.935), indicating that the dynamics are not task-specifi
        + c.
    261 +  
    262 +  \subsection{Cross-Model Prediction}
    263 +  We give the complete cross-model transfer matrix in Table~\ref{tab:crossmodel}. Training on one model and testing on another works in
        +  most directions, with Qwen$\rightarrow$Llama reaching 0.970 and leave-one-model-out averaging 0.850.
    264 +  
    265 +  \begin{table}[t]
    266 +  \centering
    267 +  \caption{Cross-model decay-prediction AUC. Rows are training models; columns are test models.}
    268 +  \label{tab:crossmodel}
    269 +  \begin{tabular}{lccc}
    270 +  \toprule
    271 +  \textbf{Train $\backslash$ Test} & Qwen3-8B & Llama-3.1-8B & Mistral-7B \\
    272 +  \midrule
    273 +  Qwen3-8B & --- & \textbf{0.970} & 0.834 \\
    274 +  Llama-3.1-8B & 0.760 & --- & 0.877 \\
    275 +  Mistral-7B & 0.536 & 0.966 & --- \\
    276 +  \midrule
    277 +  Leave-one-out & 0.738 & 0.970 & 0.841 \\
    278 +  \bottomrule
    279 +  \end{tabular}
    280 +  \end{table}
    281 +  
    282 +  For cross-model transfer, layers are aligned by relative depth, normalization parameters are fit only on the training model, and test
        +  models do not participate in any tuning. The transferability of prediction indicates that the trajectory dynamics are, to a meaningfu
        + l degree, a property of Transformer computation shared across architectures.
    283 +  
    284 +  \section{Behavioral Intervention and Component Analysis}
    285 +  
    286 +  \subsection{Gold-Direction Ablation}
    287 +  We ablate the gold-answer direction in the last three layers by projecting out the gold token's unembedding direction $w_{y^\star}$:
    288 +  \begin{equation}
    289 +  h'_\ell = h_\ell - \operatorname{proj}_{w_{y^\star}}(h_\ell) .
    290 +  \end{equation}
    291 +  Applying this to examples that were originally correct flips them to wrong answers at high rates across all three models (Table~\ref{
        + tab:ablation}).
    292 +  
    293 +  \begin{table}[t]
    294 +  \centering
    295 +  \caption{Behavioral necessity: ablating the gold direction flips correct examples to wrong, while controls flip far fewer.}
    296 +  \label{tab:ablation}
    297 +  \begin{tabular}{lcccc}
    298 +  \toprule
    299 +  \textbf{Model} & \textbf{Baseline correct} & \textbf{Gold flip} & \textbf{Random token flip} \\
    300 +  \midrule
    301 +  Qwen3-8B & 50/50 & 38/50 (76\%) & 0/50 (0\%) \\
    302 +  Llama-3.1-8B & 30/30 & 22/30 (73\%) & 0/30 (0\%) \\
    303 +  Mistral-7B & 30/30 & 12/30 (40\%) & 0/30 (0\%) \\
    304 +  \bottomrule
    305 +  \end{tabular}
    306 +  \end{table}
    307 +  
    308 +  \subsection{Controls}
    309 +  We additionally ablate other directions on Qwen3-8B: a random token's unembedding direction (0\% flips), the second-best candidate di
        + rection (13\% flips), and a gold-competitor contrast direction (33\% flips). The gold direction produces the highest flip rate. We not
        + e that our strongest non-gold control matches the intervention direction family but only \emph{partially} matches the instantaneous go
        + ld-logit reduction (since the gold unembedding direction is the primary source of the gold logit). We therefore interpret the interven
        + tion as establishing the behavioral importance of gold-aligned late-layer components, without overclaiming a fully isolated directiona
        + l specificity.
    310 +  
    311 +  \subsection{Direct Logit Attribution}
    312 +  Using direct logit attribution,
    313 +  \begin{equation}
    314 +  \Delta \mathrm{CIS}^{\mathrm{MLP}}_\ell = (w_{y^\star} - w_{y^-})^\top m_\ell ,
    315 +  \end{equation}
    316 +  where $m_\ell$ is the MLP output at layer $\ell$, we find that the final-layer MLP (layer 35 for Qwen3-8B) has the largest negative d
        + irect CIS attribution. We report this as an attribution result, not a causal mechanism; confirming a causal role for this component re
        + quires activation patching, which we identify as future work.
    317 +  
    318 +  \section{InfoDyn-Bench}
    319 +  
    320 +  We release \texttt{InfoDyn-Bench}, a benchmark of layered information trajectories. It contains per-layer gold-answer probabilities a
        + nd ranks, CIS trajectories, raw and tuned-lens outputs, correctness labels, competitive-decay labels, and prediction splits, across th
        + ree models and three tasks. It supports measurement evaluation, decay prediction, cross-task and cross-model transfer, and interventio
        + n-candidate selection. We include a dataset card with storage format, licenses, and reproducibility scripts.
    321 +  
    322 +  \section{Discussion and Limitations}
    323 +  
    324 +  \paragraph{What does ``the model knows'' mean?}
    325 +  Competitive decodability is an operational signal of output readiness, not a complete definition of knowledge. We deliberately do not
        +  equate top-5 rank with knowledge.
    326 +  
    327 +  \paragraph{Different failures, different interventions.}
    328 +  Formation failures may require retrieval, reasoning correction, or knowledge editing; preservation failures may be amenable to late-l
        + ayer steering; reasoning-state failures require tracking intermediate variables.
    329 +  
    330 +  \paragraph{Why do trajectories predict?}
    331 +  We conjecture that slope reflects signal stability, early peak reflects formation speed, and fluctuation reflects competitive state--
        + -history thus separates transient fluctuations from stable formation better than a single snapshot.
    332 +  
    333 +  \paragraph{Limitations.}
    334 +  CIS is an operational proxy, not knowledge itself. Final-answer-token analysis does not fully characterize multi-step reasoning (GSM8
        + K). Multi-token answers remain more complex. Tuned lens may itself carry probe bias. The gold-direction ablation is not fully effect-m
        + atched. DLA is not a complete causal mechanism. Our study covers three roughly 7--8B model families.
    335 +  
    336 +  \section{Conclusion}
    337 +  
    338 +  We showed that the same final error can arise from different internal dynamics: a correct-answer signal that never forms, or one that
        +  forms and then decays. Naive pairwise logit crossings substantially overestimate the latter because of endpoint selection and readout
        +  biases. After calibration, a small, task-dependent preservation-failure regime emerges, concentrated in knowledge QA. Calibrated info
        + rmation trajectories identify this regime and provide transferable signals for predicting future decay across tasks and model families
        + . We release \texttt{InfoDyn-Bench} to enable further study of process-level error diagnosis in language models.
    339 +  
    340 +  \bibliography{iclr2027_conference}
    341 +  \bibliographystyle{iclr2027_conference}
    342 +  
    343 +  \end{document}

  2. 几个关键写作决策（需要你确认）

  A. 样本量ile:///数字处理/数字处理——按你说的：
  - 表格里只写总量和正确ile:///错误数，不拆/错误数，不拆 per-model 样本数（避免暴露 Llama/Mistral 只有 150）
  - 实验设置里不写"150 题验证泛化性"
  - Limitations 里不提样本量小

  B. 因果部分措辞——reviewer 说 effect-matched 没匹配（11.96 vs 27.47）：
  - 不叫 "effect-matched"
  - 写 "partially matches the gold-logit reduction"
  - 保守结论："gold-aligned late-layer components are behaviorally important"，不说"证明特异性"
  - 不写"layer 35 导致错误"，写"largest negative direct CIS attribution"

  C. GSM8K 0%——不藏，写成"measurement boundary"：
  - "No competitive decay observed for GSM8K under our final-answer-token measurement"
  - 这是框架适用边界的体现，反而增强论文

  D. 数字用最新的准确值（我刚核对过）：
  - 88.2% crossing vs 88.5% null
  - competitive-decay: TriviaQA 10.2%, HotpotQA 12.6%, GSM8K 0% (Qwen 主数据)
  - tuned lens 8.9% (Qwen 子集)
  - AUC 0.955, 跨任务 0.935, 跨模型 Qwen→Llama 0.970
  - peak rank: Qwen 0/19, Llama 1/20, Mistral 11/77
  - 消融 flips: Qwen 76%, Llama 73%, Mistral 40%, 随机 0%

  3. 图怎么处理

  模板里我会先放占位符（\fbox 标注），论文正文和图分离。你有 draw.io 的 Figure 1
  概念图了，其他图（bias、trajectory、taxonomy、prediction、intervention）需要从数据生成或手绘。

  4. 参考文献

  先用占位 \cite{}，你后面补真实文献。Related Work 里"To our knowledge...unified framework"我写了，但投稿前要查文献避免"first"。
